Document 234: Capital Market Outlook (2026-09-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-09-14).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
While some academics have claimed the Singularity is still off in the distant future, or even
unlikely at all, those on the front lines of AI model development have recently asserted that it
has already begun. For example, Elon Musk has pointed to recent issues with AI models going

outside their proscribed limits, including hacking external systems and completing previously
unsolved math problems. Over a year ago, Sam Altman wrote, "We are past the event horizon; the
takeoff has started. Humanity is close to building superintelligence..." In short, the rapid evolution
of these models has convinced knowledgeable observers that the Singularity has begun.
Superintelligence is the primary driver of the Singularity as exponential advances in AI ultimately

create an artificial Superintelligence that is much smarter and faster than the collective
intelligence of humanity. As such, it can solve practical problems that remain unsolved by humans
and do it in a timely fashion. Superabundance is the logical implication of Superintelligence
as it works to create a world where economic goods, services and energy are essentially free
because of the omniscience implied by the most efficient processing of unlimited information by

superintelligent models. In essence, Superintelligence could mimic the Creation.
Some aspects of Superabundance include automated production of desired output that
eliminates most physical labor, clean energy and synthetic materials that eliminate resource
scarcity, and increased longevity as Superintelligence is applied to maintaining physical health
via, for example, synthetic organ replacement and personalized illness treatments. Obviously,
Superabundance pulls the rug out from under traditional economics, which is the study of how

scarce resources are allocated. While the "scarcity" vanishes in this new world, the allocation issue
remains and is likely to become the focus of political debate. In particular, the question of how
jobs and incomes fit into a superabundant world is likely to become a bigger and bigger issue.
The trend of technology replacing more and more of the labor share of income is already decades
old and dates back to the beginnings of digital technology. That trend has picked up speed

with the increasing profit share of output the new technologies created. The ratio of income
to the number of workers is orders of magnitude higher for the big new technology companies
compared to the old economy companies of the past. This means the owners of capital
(shareholders) are receiving a bigger share of the economic pie as high-margin, high-productivity
companies based in the new technology become a bigger part of the economy. This trend is

accelerating. That is a direct implication of the movement toward and through the Singularity.
It means the issue of income inequality, which has always been on society's radar screen, will
become bigger in the years ahead unless there are wise policies to share the superabundance
of the near future. Some countries are better prepared for this future than others. Norway,
for example, has built a sovereign wealth fund (SWF) over the past 25 years that represents a

substantial share of capital income for each of its citizens. In the U.S., with its widespread equity
culture, thanks to Individual Retirement Accounts and 401K plans, capital income is more widely
distributed compared to Europe with its much more debt-based capital markets. In any event,
widespread equity ownership is one way to deal with a superabundant economy where AI has
significantly reduced the labor share of income.
Demographics add to the natural evolution from labor income to capital income as societies

become wealthier. Aging societies of retirees by definition depend on capital income more than
labor income. This transition of the economy to higher margins and faster earnings growth is
the fortunate accompaniment to the need for more income from capital as societies become
older. The longevity trend naturally accelerates this process, which is the logical result of
technological progress raising productivity and living standards. People live longer in wealthy

societies, as subsistence constraints on life spans diminish while lifestyle and mental health
constraints become more the issue. How lifestyles and mental health evolve in a world where
work becomes less necessary is already showing signs of their increased importance for the
future of public health.
The current accelerating profits cycle reflects this impact of new technology on the relative
returns to labor and capital. The trend so far has been relatively slow compared to what's

coming if Superintelligence and Superabundance are the outcomes the Singularity promises as
increasingly seems likely. Because much financial analysis relies on past cycles the accelerating
Investment Implications
Rapid innovation, political upheaval
and increasing creative destruction
raise the difficulty of valuing
longer duration assets as we saw
earlier this year in the software
space. Higher-risk premia are a
likely result. On balance, this favors

Value-oriented assets over Growth.
Higher real yields in a strong capital
spending environment reinforce this
preference.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
profits and rising margins of the current economy have naturally focused attention on what
happens when conditions revert to the mean of lower margins and more historically normal
profit growth. Looked at through this lens, Equities appear overvalued and destined to correct
bubble-like conditions. The Singularity, however, implies "this time is different," the four most
dangerous words in investing. Yet, the slowly evolving shift toward capital income already under

way is an early warning sign of bigger things to come as these trends are turbo-charged to warp
speed while the economy passes through the Singularity. Seen this way, a super-cycle in profits,
margins and stock values seems logical.
A revamping of the social safety net to address the conditions implied by Superabundance
is already a hot topic in Silicon Valley. Various proposals for a universal basic income (UBI)
are being bandied about. Typically, UBI refers to the government giving everyone an income

sufficient to meet some living standard. This automatically implies a big role for government in
the new economy. Given the fourth-turning breakdown in the political process and governance,
alternatives that avoid the government sector might also be considered. For example, another
implication of the Singularity is an accelerating accumulation of wealth fueling a growing supply
of altruistic and philanthropic potential that arguably could be administered more efficiently by

the private sector than the government where widespread allegations of corrupt administration
at all levels have proliferated since the pandemic. Given the growing wealth pie and the diversity
of social priorities among its owners addressing more social needs through philanthropy is a
likely option for the superabundant economy. In any event, the relative roles of government and
the private sector are necessarily an issue to be hashed out in this revolutionary time.

More glimmers of the UBI-like movements have popped up recently. As mentioned, Norway's
SWF is an example of how a well-administered government has been preparing for this future.
In the U.S. government sponsored tax-advantaged savings accounts from birth have been
instituted with private donations being added to the program. Governments, including the U.S.,
have increasingly been buying stakes in private investments for various purposes with the result

that the focus solely on sovereign debt can be a misleading indicator of fiscal fragility.
Japan is a significant example of the difference between gross government debt and net
government debt after subtracting government owned assets. Its gross debt is around 279% of
its gross domestic product (GDP) which is much higher than any other major economy. However,
Japan government assets total around 192% of GDP , including domestic and foreign Equities,

bonds, and loans. As a result, its net debt is less than 100% of GDP and more in line with other
major economies. As one of the most aged societies in the world, Japan points the way toward
how societies can cope with longevity. Government ownership of a broad array of higher yielding
global assets funded with low sovereign debt interest rates appears to be a path the U.S.
Treasury is also starting to pursue in managing its debt burden.
The main risks to the uptopian promise of Superabundance are the dystopian possibilities

that would follow from poor social decision-making. Around the world, different governments
will follow different approaches to navigating the unprecedented challenges posed by the
Singularity. As the U.S. celebrates its 250-year anniversary, it is in the midst of its fourth "fourth
turning." These are the intense political upheaval periods that demographers and historians have
documented in every fourth generation. The Revolutionary War, the Civil War, and the Great

Depression and World War II mark the first three "fourth turnings." The Singularity is a major
marker for the current fourth turning.
The relative success of the U.S. in transitioning through the previous major turning points in
history provides hope for a utopian rather than a dystopian outcome. Other countries have not
transited these challenges so well for such an extended period which helps explain how the
U.S. has become the most prosperous country on earth. Nevertheless, past performance is no

guarantee of future success. As always, each generation faces new challenges to overcome, or not.
For investors, the Singularity seems to be behind an unusually strong earnings cycle that could
potentially last much longer than past history would suggest. That's positive for Equities and
risk assets in general. The extra risks associated with this rapid transformation argue for well
diversified holdings as creative destruction on steroids seems a plausible outcome in a world of

warp speed change.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Fixed Income Strategy Team
For much of the past two decades, rising debt levels appeared to carry few
consequences. Federal deficits expanded and corporate borrowing increased, yet
Treasury yields generally trended lower and credit spreads stayed remarkably tame.
Today, the backdrop may be changing.

Federal borrowing needs continue to grow as deficit spending remains elevated. Net
interest expense has surpassed $1 trillion annually and now rivals total U.S. defense
spending. On this front there appears no end in sight as the Congressional Budget
Office projects that the fiscal 2026 shortfall will widen to roughly $2.1 trillion. Investors
are increasingly focused on not only the level of debt outstanding, but also the pace at
which new debt is being issued—with some questioning about the sustainability of this

situation over the longer term.
This phenomenon is not limited to Treasurys, as the issuance glut extends to corporate
debt. Investment-grade (IG) corporate issuance is running roughly 40% above last year
and is on pace for a record year. 1 While demand for Fixed Income remains healthy, the
sheer volume of new debt being brought to market is beginning to test investor capacity,
particularly at the long end of the Treasury curve and within certain sectors.

Hyperscalers Are Competing for Long-Duration Capital. A significant driver of
the surge in corporate issuance has been the hyperscalers. The largest technology
companies are financing an unprecedented wave of AI-related capital spending, leading
to nearly $280 billion of debt issuance year-to-date. 2 Many of these issuers carry high
credit ratings and have ramped up issuance of longer-maturity bonds. As a result, they
are increasingly competing with long-dated Treasurys for the same pool of institutional

capital, including insurance companies, pension funds, sovereign wealth funds and
duration-oriented Fixed Income investors—creating a crowding-out effect amongst high-
quality Fixed Income.
Fortunately, this is a case of pressure, not a credit crisis. Recent weakness in long-dated
Treasurys reflects a classic supply-and-demand imbalance and importantly is not a
solvency crisis for the U.S. government or high-quality hyperscalers. Investors are being

asked to absorb a growing volume of net new issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate America at the same time.
Debt Pressure and a Normalization, Not a Fiscal Reckoning. The rise in long-end
yields during August reflects this dynamic. The 30-year Treasury yield moved above
5.3%, reaching levels not seen since 2007. Fiscal concerns likely played a role, but we

2 Source: Bloomberg as of September 9, 2026.

Investment Implications
The recent rise in Treasury yields
reflects a collision of fiscal concerns,
resilient growth, persistent inflation,
and record debt issuance from both
the Treasury and corporate borrowers.
While this has created periodic bouts
of market pressures, we view recent
move as a normalization process, not a
crisis of confidence. Attractive real and
nominal yields reinforce our view that
investors should look beyond cash and
short-duration assets and move closer

to strategic duration target.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
believe heavy supply, sticky inflation, reduced Fed demand and increased competition for
investor capital were equally important contributors.
And importantly, the market's adjustment mechanism is already working. Higher Treasury
yields and somewhat wider credit spreads are attracting buyers and helping clear the growing
supply of bonds. This is a natural normalization and is how markets restore equilibrium.

Recent Treasury auctions continue to attract demand consistent with historical norms, and
investor appetite for new IG issuance has remained robust. These are not characteristics
typically associated with a loss of confidence in U.S., or corporate, creditworthiness.
A New Yield Regime. For Fixed Income investors, the more important implication
is that a 4% to 5% 10-year Treasury yield may represent the new normal. In an
environment of very strong underlying economic growth and unprecedented volumes of

debt issuance from both the public and private sectors, higher yields may be required to
attract sufficient capital amid a more competitive landscape for yield.
We continue to view both nominal and real yields as attractive and believe investors
should remain focused on achieving strategic duration targets. While rate volatility may
persist, today's yield levels provide a potential opportunity to seek attractive income that
has been largely absent for much of the past decade.

The Bigger Picture: Growth Still Matters More Than Debt Levels. None of this dismisses
the long-term fiscal challenges facing the U.S. Debt levels are rising, interest costs
continue to grow, and mandatory spending increasingly consumes a larger share of the
federal budget. However, history shows that debt levels alone do not determine interest
rates. Between 2007 and 2020, U.S. debt rose dramatically even as the 10-year Treasury
yield fell from roughly 5% to below 1%. What ultimately matters is the interplay between

short-term interest rates, debt levels, economic growth, inflation and investor demand.
For instance, nominal GDP growth and the 10-year yield tend to be correlated, outside
of recessions—we believe recent rate increases partly reflect this strong macro
backdrop. Moreover, economic growth is running above long-end yields, supporting the
sustainability of rising government debt.

The U.S. continues to benefit from several structural advantages: the dollar remains
the world's reserve currency, Treasurys remain the foundation of the global financial

system, and U.S. capital markets are still the deepest and most liquid in the world. Those
advantages continue to support demand for U.S. assets and underpin sovereign credit
quality even as borrowing needs grow.
The bottom line, in our view, is that even though the recent rise in long-end yields catches
many headlines, there is no need to panic. Instead, the recent repricing reflects periodic
episodes of debt pressure rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in U.S. credit. With

borrowing demand from both Washington and corporate America likely to remain elevated,
bond prices may continue to serve as the market's release valve. The consequence is likely
a world of structurally higher long-term yields, not a sovereign debt crisis.

6 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are the markets actually telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
When the markets speak loudly we should listen. There are often clues in the internal trends
within the markets that help us determine how far into an economic cycle we are or what drivers
of growth are most impactful. In addition, we can also find clues that help with determining

the micro picture. In other words, what industries are the most likely to benefit, from a
profitability standpoint is concerned, as the growth picture unfolds? Currently, the markets have
a megaphone, in our view. The year-to-date sharp outperformers have been consistent and
should continue to advance. These include commodities, including copper and oil, Emerging
Markets, Japan in the Developed Markets, Small-cap relative to Large-cap shares, Value versus

Growth, and more cyclically-oriented companies versus more defensive. This current consistent
outperformance informs us that we are still early- to mid-cycle in a supply-led expansion. We are
still early in the build out of the supply that is needed for the natural demand that is there. New
infrastructure is needed to handle the new economy that is progressing and quickly innovating.
This infrastructure needs commodities, all forms of energy, battery storage, minerals, metals,

recycled water, gases, chemicals, and all the compute and cloud capacity, including memory
and other components to handle the path toward mass tokenization. Moreover, the buildup of
government debt in the advanced economies as fiscal deficits widen is forcing large institutional
investors (including central banks and other government entities) to diversify their reserve
bases. This has increased the interest in gold holdings. Countries that have a vast amount of

natural resources, especially oil and gas, are in high demand by investors, which is obvious when
examining year-to-date outperformers. The bottom line is that the markets are telling us that we
are still early- to mid-cycle in this massive buildout, which will require a wide collection of hard
assets, Infrastructure (old and new), land, and within Equities, a more diversified approach to
exposure—particularly areas that are more cyclical- and Value-oriented. We expect this view to

extend itself well into the foreseeable future if not years.
In Fixed Income, it may seem more complex, but it is actually easier to understand what the
markets are telling us, in our opinion. The backup in yields is “real yield” driven, which is more due
to higher nominal growth than to cconcerns of long-run inflation. This is evident in the Treasury
inflation protection market, break-evens in general and the ratio of Treasury yields relative to

other Government bond markets in the developed world. However, rising oil and gas prices and
inflation gauges that are still above the preferred level are usually the excuses for yields backing
up. Deficit concerns are a more plausible reason for yields rising but the main driver, in our view,
is still above average nominal growth. The yield curve and rates in general are still adjusting to
this new economic landscape that is very different than those of the most recent past.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bloomberg Commodity Index designed to provide liquid and diversified exposure to commodities via futures contracts. Is calculated on an excess return basis and reflects commodity
futures price movements.
MSCI Emerging Markets Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets countries. With 1,205 constituents, the index covers approximately 85% of the free
float adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI Japan Index is designed to measure the performance of the large and mid cap segments of the Japanese market.

MSCI Pacific ex Japan Index captures large and mid cap representation across 4 of 5 Developed Markets (DM) countries in the Pacific region (excluding Japan). With 93 constituents, the index
covers approximately 85% of the free float-adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.

MSCI World Index ex U.S. captures large and mid cap representation across 22 of 23 Developed Markets countries--excluding the United States..
MSCI USA Momentum Index is based on MSCI USA Index, its parent index, which captures large and mid-cap stocks of the U.S. market. It is designed to reflect the performance of an equity
momentum strategy by emphasizing stocks with high price momentum, while maintaining reasonably high trading liquidity, investment capacity and moderate index turnover.

U.S. Large-cap Growth/Russell 1000 Growth Index measures the performance of the large-cap growth segment of the U.S. equity universe. It includes those Russell 1000 companies with
relatively higher price-to-book ratios, higher I/B/E/S forecast medium term (2 year) growth and higher sales per share historical growth (5 years).
U.S. Small-cap/Russell 2000 Index measures the performance of the small-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe.
MSCI USA High Dividend Yield Index is designed to reflect the performance of equities in the MSCI USA Index (excluding REIT s) with higher dividend income and quality characteristics than

average dividend yields that are both sustainable and persistent.
U.S. Mid Cap/Russell Midcap Index measures the performance of the mid-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe. The Russell Midcap Index is a subset of the Russell 1000® Index. It
includes approximately 800 of the smallest securities based on a combination of their market cap and current index membership.
MSCI USA Value Index captures large and mid-cap U.S. securities exhibiting overall value style characteristics.

MSCI USA Equal Weighted Index represents an alternative weighting scheme to its market cap weighted parent index, the MSCI USA Index. At each quarterly rebalance date, all index
constituents are weighted equally, effectively removing the influence of each constituent’s current price.
MSCI USA Minimum Volatility Index aims to reflect the performance characteristics of a minimum variance strategy applied to the large and mid-cap USA equity universe.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Document 238: Cliff Notes_ seeking to instil trust (2026-09-20)

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: seeking to instil trust
By Elliot Clarke, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
10:20 September 18 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
As expected, after last week’s US CPI confirmed an unwelcome degree of persistence in consumer
inflation, the FOMC voted to raise the fed funds rate by 25bps to a mid-point of 3.875%. Updated
Committee forecasts were broadly in line with market expectations, with a second hike expected by

year end and 8 of 18 contributors to the dot plot anticipating a third hike in 2027. Chair Warsh also
focused attention on the risks to the inflation outlook during the press conference.

### Page 2

The Committee’s PCE inflation forecasts are little changed since June despite today’s rate hike and the
additional tightening forecast (from 3.7%yr in 2026 to 2.3%yr in 2027, then 2.1%yr in 2028). This profile
implies the FOMC feels a need to act against renewed inflation risks now, but believes policy will prove
effective in time. Limiting concern over the implications of tighter policy for activity is the Committee’s
entrenched belief in the underlying health of the US economy, represented in September’s forecasts by

GDP growth remaining above trend over the forecast horizon and the unemployment rate holding at a
level consistent with full employment.
Westpac is more cautious on downside risks to the US labour market and growth, but only at the
margin. We also believe it will take time for headline annual inflation to slow given geopolitical
uncertainty and the limited interest rate sensitivity of domestic US inflation, most obvious in the shelter
component. Another rate increase by year end should give market participants confidence that policy

will prove effective within the forecast window. The Committee is likely to stand ready to deliver a third
hike, but this is less than a 50/50 chance on current data, in our view. Rate cuts will not be seen until
2028, however, and they are likely to only reverse the current tightening sequence, with limited spare
capacity in the US economy outside the tech sector biasing inflation above 2.0%yr in to the medium
term.
In contrast to the FOMC’s September decision, in the UK the Bank of England decided to hold Bank

Rate at 3.75% in a 6-3 decision. The Committee is cognisant that headline inflation is well above target
at 3.1%yr, but holds this as almost entirely due to energy prices, with "little evidence of significant
knock-on effects on prices and wages". That said, the longer the conflict persists, the higher the
likelihood of secondary inflation effects for the consumer, requiring tighter monetary policy. Also arguing
for a measured and careful response to the current inflationary threat is that, absent the crisis, the

Bank of England was projected to have been easing through 2026. Monetary policy is therefore tighter
than it would have otherwise been, with implications for both inflation and activity. We expect the
Committee will decide to hike into year end, but likely only once.
Coming back to Asia, China’s August data round (yet) again highlighted the need for urgent pro-active
stimulus at scale to restore sentiment and bolster domestic demand. Annual growth in retail sales

slowed to just 0.4% in August, while the year-to-date decline in fixed asset investment accelerated from
-6.7% to -7.2%, with property investment down 19.9%. New and existing home prices fell another 0.2%
and 0.3% in the month, continuing the trend of recent years.
As highlighted in our September Market Outlook, consumers and local businesses in China have the
capacity to increase spending, but they are reticent to do so. Confidence must be restored, built on a

belief that income and wealth will grow consistently and equitably, not just for those directly connected
to the technology trade.

Economy 
Online betting platforms: What’s the money line? 
01 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• Online betting adoption is accelerating. According to Bank of America consumer account data, the number of online betting 
users rose sharply this summer, with first-time users in June and July more than triple January levels. Activity is concentrated 
among younger consumers, with Gen Z and Millennials accounting for 88% of online betting activity in July. 
• Based on observed inflows and outflows to these platforms, customer inflows averaged less than three-quarters of outflows 
during the period analyzed. While these figures may not capture winnings that remain on platforms, they suggest online betting 
is not currently functioning as a meaningful source of income for most bettors. 
• In 2026, the median deposit account balance of households that participated in online betting was 59% of households that did 
not. With prediction markets rapidly expanding alongside traditional sports betting, this has prompted some to blur the lines 
between entertainment and investment, according to a Bank of America proprietary survey.

Place your bets – increasingly online 
Increasingly, online betting appears to be gaining adoption. In this analysis, we identify online betting payments (automated 
clearing house (ACH), debit card and credit card) to and from companies that offer online betting platforms – namely prediction 
markets and sports betting. Note that this excludes online social gambling from typical casinos or other gaming platforms. See 
Methodology for more details. 
According to Bank of America customer account data, around 5% of customers participated in online betting in July. Yet 
consumer adoption of online betting platforms has been rising rapidly and was up 40% in July compared to the start of the year 
(Exhibit 1). Perhaps more notably, the number of first-time (i.e. someone with a first-time payment to one of these platforms) 
online betting users jumped more than 3x that of January users in June and July. See Methodology for first-time users. 
Exhibit 1: In June and July, the number of first-time online betting users jumped around 3x that of the start of the year 
Online betting users (number of customers, index January 2026 = 100, monthly)

Source: Bank of America internal data

Sports calendar drives spending 
Some of the June/July peak was likely driven in part by promotions and buzz related to prediction markets as well as the FIFA 
World Cup 2026™ (read more on the economic impact of the World Cup in August’s On the ball). Our analysis suggests online 
betting activity appears quite volatile and predominantly tied to the timing of major sporting events.  
For example, the growth in first-time users is apparent in both the basketball and football seasons (both college and professional 
leagues), with the latter having seen 22% year-over-year (YoY) growth in the 2025 season (Exhibit 2). Football season is defined 
as September through February and therefore overlaps with other major league sports such as baseball and hockey. 
According to a recent CivicScience survey, over a third of those who partake in online sports betting did so weekly and almost a 
quarter placed bets daily (Exhibit 3). Women were less likely to report doing so frequently (i.e. daily or weekly) compared to men. 
Exhibit 2: First-time online betting users grew 22% YoY in the 2025

Gen Z doubles down 
Who is placing the bets? According to Bank of America consumer account data, across income terciles, online betting activity is 
relatively evenly split, although lower-income households comprised a slightly larger share of online betting activity than other 
income groups (Exhibit 4). 
However, by generation, online betting activity within Bank of America consumer account data is heavily skewed towards Gen Z 
and Millennials. In July, they comprised 88% of online betting activity (Exhibit 5). Notably, until June, Millennials accounted for a 
larger share of activity, suggesting Gen Z initiated a turning point in the composition of online bettors this summer.

I NSTI TUTE

The concentration of betting activity among Gen Z may reflect broader shifts toward app-based financial and entertainment 
experiences (read more on Gen Z behavior in August’s Gen Z reality check). Younger consumers have generally been quicker to 
adopt emerging digital platforms, including crypto, buy now pay later (BNPL) products and online marketplaces (read more on 
online marketplace adoption in April’s Secondhand fashion piece). 
Are the odds in your favor? 
With more consumers increasing their online betting activity, are they also bringing in extra income? Using Bank of America 
payments data, we measure the inflow-to-outflow payment ratio of customers placing bets. It is important to note that we can 
only see money deposited to and from these platforms each month and therefore may not capture winnings that remain on 
betting platforms and have not yet been withdrawn.  
However, our analysis found the online betting cash recovery ratio has remained below 1, with total inflows less than three-
quarters of total outflows on average for the duration of the series (Exhibit 6). In other words, customers typically recover less 
than 75 cents for every dollar transferred to online betting platforms.  
Exhibit 6: The online betting cash recovery ratio has remained below 1, with total inflows less than three-quarters that of total outflows on average 
for the duration of the series 
Online betting cash recovery ratio (monthly, 2026)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
Note: A ratio less than 1 means inflow was less than outflow. These estimates should be interpreted as observed inflows and outflows to betting platforms rather than realized profitability, since winnings 
retained on-platform are not fully observable. 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Does Gen Z see online betting as a source of income? 
Although Gen Z participants had the highest online betting cash recovery ratio across income groups, they still fell well short of 
breaking even (Exhibit 7). Despite seemingly recovering more than older generations, total inflows remained substantially below 
total outflows, suggesting that online betting is not a reliable or constant source of income. 
Exhibit 7: While Gen Z still recovers less of what they spend, older generations have the lowest cash recovery ratio

20% consider sports betting as a type of investment 
Despite the lack of profitability, investing and betting appears increasingly blurred for some consumers. Prediction markets, 
crypto assets, retail trading and sports betting all share common features such as community participation and real-time pricing. 
According to a Bank of America proprietary survey, 20% of respondents consider sports betting as a type of investment, 
although more consider it not a form of investing (Exhibit 8). Gen Z is twice as likely to consider sports betting as a form of 
investing; however, across all generations, buying event contracts on prediction markets was more likely to be considered an 
investment than sports betting.  
Exhibit 8: Gen Z is more likely to consider buying event contracts on prediction markets or sports betting as a non-traditional form of investing 
Classifications of investing activities (% of respondents by generation)

Source: Bank of America proprietary survey 
Note: Survey conducted March 24-31, 2026 across 2,351 respondents. 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The financial spread widens 
Does the financial health playbook look different between households who gamble versus those who don’t? Yes, although some 
of this comes down to demographics. 
Following the 2018 Murphy v. NCAA Supreme Court decision opening the door for states to legalize sports betting, according to 
a working paper from the National Bureau of Economic Research, as legalization went into effect, sports betting spread quickly, 
with both the number of participants and frequency of bets increasing over time. This increase did not displace other gambling 
or consumption but significantly reduced savings, as risky bets crowd out positive expected value investments.1  
That same paper found these effects were concentrated among financially constrained households. Bank of America customer 
deposit account data found that of those households that participate in online betting in 2026, their median deposit account 
balances were only 59% of those who did not (Exhibit 9).  
In addition to having lower median deposit balances, households with online betting activity have stronger total card spending 
growth excluding online betting (Exhibit 10). These households also had stronger spending growth in July across both 
discretionary and necessity items, though this could also be because younger and lower-income households spending growth has 
strengthened recently (read more on this in the August Consumer Checkpoint).

1 Baker, S.R., Balthrop, J., Johnson, M.J., Kotter, J.D., & Pisciotta, K. (2024, November). Gambling Away Stability: Sports Betting’s Impact on Vulnerable 
Households. National Bureau of Economic Research.

Policymakers enter the game with a possible new set of rules 
While sports betting remains the dominant area within online betting, broader prediction markets appear to be attracting 
increasing consumer attention too. According to BofA Global Public Policy, wagers have been placed (or markets have been 
created) on almost everything: whether a political speaker will drink water on camera during a speech, the total flu cases 
predicted for the year, etc. These features have raised the question of whether such instruments function more like financial 
contracts or resemble forms of wagering under state law.  
Prediction markets operate by listing standardized contracts – often referred to as event contracts – that pay out based on 
whether a specified outcome occurs (Exhibit 11). According to BofA Global Public Policy, the Commodities Futures Trading 
Commission (CFTC) argues that certain prediction market event contracts, when traded on federally regulated exchanges, 
constitute derivatives (classified as commodity interests under the Commodity Exchange Act (CEA)) and are therefore subject to 
federal oversight. 
Exhibit 11: Prediction markets vary from both sports betting and other financial contracts

I NSTI TUTE

CFTC creates rulemaking around incentive programs 
The CFTC recently implemented rulemaking around perpetual futures contracts (i.e. speculating an asset's price with no 
expiration date) and put out an advisory regarding incentive programs for event contract trading. Within this, the CFTC warned 
against sweepstakes-like or randomized reward programs, or prizes based on pure chance. 
Growing concerns about the rapid rise of prediction markets have prompted lawmakers from both parties and chambers of 
Congress to introduce several bills aimed at establishing clearer federal rules, particularly around consumer protections. At the 
same time, many states and tribal regulators argue that some event contracts, especially those tied to sports and entertainment, 
closely resemble traditional gambling and should remain subject to existing state gaming, licensing and consumer protection 
laws. 
According to BofA Global Research, the CFTC is aiming to make clearer distinctions between sports books and prediction 
markets highlighted by recent rule proposals and advisories regarding in-house market making, incentive programs and casino 
style odds.

I NSTI TUTE

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Bank of America credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card holders 
making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards is excluded. Data regarding 
merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial services 
companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System (NAICS), 
which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
If applicable, the consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level.  
If applicable, any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a 
deposit account or credit card. Any reference to aggregated AI spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, or bill pay.  
Online betting transactions conducted through Bank of America debit and credit card or ACH channels and online betting platforms are 
identified using industry-researched merchant names. Note that the scope of this series is limited to predominantly online sports betting, horse 
racing, and prediction markets. 
First-time users are defined as customers making their first observed transaction in the category during the particular month, based on 
transaction records dating back to 2015. 
Football season (defined as September through February) was selected as a measure as this captures a sizeable amount of online betting 
activity. 
The measures in this report reflect customer payment flows to and from identified online betting platforms and should not be interpreted as a 
comprehensive measure of gambling activity, betting volume, customer profitability, or platform market share. 
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
A Bank of America Market Insights Survey was conducted between March 24-31, 2026, among 2,351 respondents. 
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 1

Article | 18 September 2026 1
THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article  |  18 September 2026   SWITZERLAND
Swiss National Bank preview: Still no
reason to move
Swiss economic growth has recently surprised on the upside, but
inflation remains too low to warrant a change in monetary policy. We
expect the Swiss National Bank to keep its policy rate at 0% next
Thursday and to remain on hold over the coming quarters
Swiss National Bank Chairman Martin Schlegel

The economy has performed better than expected
The Swiss economy has recently delivered a positive surprise. GDP adjusted for sporting events
increased by a particularly strong 1.5% quarter-on-quarter in the second quarter. Importantly,
this figure is not annualised, making the expansion all the more remarkable. Growth was
broad-based across sectors and demand components, in line with the improvement in
confidence indicators.
The headline figure nevertheless probably overstates the underlying strength of the economy.

Almost half of the quarterly increase came from the volatile chemicals and pharmaceuticals
sector, alongside a sharp rise in exports. A deceleration is therefore likely in the second half of
the year.
Even so, the economy is clearly performing better than anticipated a few months ago. We

### Page 2

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 18 September 2026 2
have raised our forecast for GDP growth to an average of 1.9% in 2026, while we expect
growth of 1.6% in 2027. The stronger outlook reflects the solid performance recorded in the
first half of the year, somewhat more favourable international demand and the recent slight
weakening of the Swiss franc. Domestic demand should also continue to expand, supported by
private consumption and stronger capacity utilisation.

Stronger growth is not an inflation risk
The stronger growth performance does not, in our view, represent a significant risk to price
stability. The consequences of the global energy shock are visible in Switzerland, but they
remain much less severe than in most other developed economies.
Following the rise in global energy prices, headline inflation increased to 0.8% year-on-year in
August, from 0.4% in July. This was largely driven by petroleum product prices, which were

25.2% higher than a year earlier. Nevertheless, inflation remains comfortably within the SNB’s
0-2% inflation target.
More importantly, underlying inflationary pressures remain extremely weak. Excluding
petroleum products, Swiss inflation stood at just 0.3% year-on-year in August. This suggests
that the energy shock has not translated into broad-based price pressures and that domestic
second-round effects remain limited.
The strength of the Swiss franc continues to play a considerable role in keeping inflation below

that of Switzerland’s main trading partners. By limiting the increase in the price of imported
goods, the currency acts as an important buffer against global inflationary shocks. This
mechanism is particularly powerful in Switzerland, where imported goods account for around
22% of the consumer price index. It also helps explain why the current energy shock is proving
much less inflationary than elsewhere, as was already the case in 2022.
However, the exchange-rate channel provided slightly less disinflationary support in August

than in previous months. The franc has weakened moderately against the euro since the SNB’s
June meeting. This should provide some support to exporters and could put limited upward
pressure on imported prices, but the currency remains strong by historical standards.
Inflation should remain under control
Inflation is likely to remain contained over the coming months. In forecasts published
yesterday, the government maintained its projections for average inflation at 0.6% in both

2026 and 2027. These forecasts are based on the current profile of energy futures, which
points to lower oil prices over the coming months. If that profile materialises, part of the recent
increase in headline inflation should prove temporary.
We believe that the SNB is likely to revise its conditional inflation forecast slightly higher,
particularly over the short term, to account for the recent increase in energy prices and the

### Page 3

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 18 September 2026 3
somewhat weaker franc. At its June meeting, it forecast average inflation of 0.6% in 2026 and
2027 and 0.7% in 2028. A modest upward adjustment now looks likely, but we do not expect a
significant revision to the medium-term profile. Underlying inflation remains very low,
domestic price pressures are contained and the pass-through from the energy shock remains
limited.
Overall, there is little to suggest that the SNB needs to change its policy rate. We expect it to

leave the rate unchanged at 0% on Thursday and to remain on hold over the coming quarters,
particularly if, as we expect, global energy prices eventually decline.
The SNB is therefore likely to continue to stand out from other central banks by maintaining a
much more accommodative monetary policy stance. This divergence reflects Switzerland’s
domestic inflation environment, which remains unusually benign thanks in large part to a
currency that is still strong.

FX intervention remains a backstop, not the base case
Foreign exchange intervention will remain part of the SNB’s policy toolkit. In June, the central
bank referred specifically to purchases of foreign currency to counter a rapid and excessive
appreciation of the franc. Such action would remain possible in the event of a renewed safe-
haven shock that pushes imported inflation lower and revives deflation risks.
For now, the franc’s recent weakening and Switzerland’s persistently lower inflation relative to

its trading partners have reduced the need to intervene. The SNB is therefore unlikely to
respond to every bout of nominal appreciation, particularly as the franc’s real effective
appreciation remains more limited.
The asymmetry of the SNB’s communication is also significant. It has not signalled a
willingness to sell foreign currency to strengthen the franc, as it did in 2022, to curb imported
inflation. With inflation still firmly under control, there is little reason to reopen the door to

such interventions. We therefore expect the SNB to retain its one-sided guidance: FX purchases
remain possible in response to an abrupt and excessive appreciation, but systematic
intervention and a return to the 2022 strategy are unlikely.
All in all, we expect the SNB to leave its policy rate at 0% and to retain its targeted
approach to foreign exchange intervention. As long as domestic inflation remains
subdued and the franc stays strong, albeit without appreciating excessively, the SNB

can continue to run a significantly more accommodative monetary policy stance than
most other central banks.

### Page 3

美联储开启近三年首轮加息
资料来源：Federal Reserve Board、方 正中 期期 货研 究院
北京时间 9月17日凌晨 2点，美联储宣布将基
准利率提升 25BP至3.75-4% 。这是近三年以
来美联 储首 次加 息 ， 上次 加息 还要 追溯 至
2023年7月27日，当时美国正值 40年一遇的大
通胀。
9月FOMC 声明继续沿用 6月和7月相对精简模
板，通过这三次议息会议可以确定这将会是
凯文 · 沃 什 执 掌 下 的 美 联 储 长 期 使 用 的
FOMC 声明模板 。相较于 7月FOMC 声明，9
月做出了多处调整：
1、美联储宣布加息 25BP符合预期 ，但全票
通过则超出预期 ，这就意味着原本在公开场
合支持 “维持利率不变 ”的纽约联储主席威
廉姆斯等多位鸽派委员也支持加息 。由此可
见，当前通胀形势严峻 ，美联储收紧货币政
策已到了刻不容缓的地步 。 鉴于 9月议息 会
议全票通过 ，因此删除了 7月FOMC 中的最后
一段，即投下反对票的委员姓名和意见 。

2、继续将不确定性归咎于地缘 ，但措辞 略
有变化 ——将Middle East 变更为 geopolitical
developments ，或许美联储认为当前地缘问题
不局限于中东。
3、新增 “国内支出一直具 有弹性 ”，暗示
本轮加息是有稳定的美国经济作为支撑 。
4、删除了 “通胀高企部分源于供给冲击 ”，
新增“今天的政策行动将有助于更及时地回
到委员会 2%的目标 ”，表明美联储坚持以迅
速达成目标为导向 ，不再过度深究通胀缘由 。

### Page 4

美联储上调经济和通胀预期 下调失业率
请务必阅读最后 重要 事项
资料来源：Federal Reserve Board、方 正中 期期 货研 究院
相较于6月《经济预测摘要 》（Summary
of Economic Projections），9月整体上调了
经济增速和通胀预期 ，下调了失业率 ，为
进一步加息做基本面铺垫。
• 经济方面：将 2026-2027 年实际GDP增
速上调 0.1BP至2.3%和2.4%，维持2028
年2.2%保持不变 ，新增2029年2.1%的预
测，长期经济增速预期维持2%不变。
• 失业方面：预计 2026 -2029 年失业率均
为4.1%，整体下修0.1-0.2BP。
• 通胀方面：将 2026 年PCE 与核心PCE增
速上修 0.1BP至3.7%和3.4%；2027 年通
胀预期大幅缓解 ，尤其总通胀率下降至
2.3%，甚至低于核心通胀率的 2.5%，这
应该是基于明年地缘局势能够显著缓和
的预期所做的预判； 2028 年PCE与核心
PCE 增速上修 0.1BP至2.1%和2.2%，新

增对于2029年PCE增速2%，可见美联储
实现2%的通 胀目标将延 后一年 ， 至少
等到2029年才有望达成。

### Page 5

点阵图中枢上移
资料来源：Federal Reserve Board、方 正中 期期 货研 究院
凯文 ·沃什继续拒绝提交个人前瞻
性意见，因此9月点阵图与 6月一样，均
只收集到18位委员的预测 。沃什希望公
众以经济、就业、通胀等基本面数据作
为未来货币路径的判断导向 ，而非揣测
美联储委员的意图。
• 2026 年：支持再加息 1轮至 4-4.25%
的委员人数占据压倒性优势 ，达到
12位； 4位委员支持再加息 2轮，2位
委员支持不再加息。
• 2027 年：利率中枢保持 4.1%不变 ，
但意见出现显著分化 。支持累计再
加息2轮至4.25-4.5%的委员人数最多 ，
达到 8位；支持累计再 加息 1轮的委
员人数也有 6位之多 。 另有 4位委员
认为可以开启降息 ，3位委员认为下
调至3.5-3.75%，1位委员认为应该大
幅下调至3-3.25%。
• 2028 年：利率中枢回落 20BP至3.9%，
意见较为 分散 ， 在“3- 3.25 %”至
“4-4.25%”五档利率区间均有分布 。

• 2029 年：利率中枢继续下移 30BP 至
3 . 6 % ， 分布于 “ 2 . 75 - 3 % ” 至
“3.75-4%”五档利率区间。
• 长期：中性利率从 3.1%上提至 3.2% 。
整体来看 ，9月点阵图相较于 6月利
率中 枢 上移 。 2026 年还 将 加息 1 轮；
2027 年意见分化严重 ，至多再加息 1轮
至4.25-4.5%；2028 年之后三年进入降
息周期；长期中性利率为3.2%。

### Page 6

沃什讲话极为鹰派 美联储加息预期升温
资料来源：Wind、 方正 中期 期货 研究 院
• 沃什首先指出此次加息正处于美国经济走强之际 ，新增招聘 、私营部门收入 、企业资本投资在最近几个月均得以改善 ，信贷流动强
劲，当前货币政策难言限制性 。强调抗通胀是目前美联储主要关注点 ，过年5年通胀率始终高于 2%长期目标 ，如今通胀高企而且潜
在趋势并未实质性改善 。沃什透露在 7月议息会议中 ，委员会就有通胀过高的共识 ，不过当时并未立即选择加息 ，为了等待更多数
据再做决定。沃什重申美联储的独立性，加息是委员会共同决定 ，未受到外部市场或政治压力的影响 。
• 相较于点阵图显示未来至多再加息两轮，市场对于加息预期更高，达到三轮。根据 CME美联储观察显示，今年四季度、明年一季
度、明年年中将各加息一轮。

### Page 7

英国央行连续六轮按兵不动
资料来源：Bloomberg、iFinD、 方正 中期 期货 研究 院
• 英国央行以 “6票赞同 、3 票反对 ”的投票结果通过了维持基准利率 3.75%不变的决议 ，这是英国央行连续 6轮按兵不动 ，3张反对票均认为应该加息 25 BP。会议纪要显委员会高
度关注通胀问题 ，8月英国通胀反弹至 3.1%，创今年以来新高 ，英国央行认为未来数月价格推升的压力可能进一步累积 ，能源冲击的间接影响更可能是延迟而非减弱 ，可能通过
企业成本 、工资和定价等渠道向经济内部传导 ，推测今年底通胀还将进一步攀升至 3.75 %。行长贝利指出全球能源冲击对英国物价和工资的直接影响迄今有限 ，但这种波动持
续的时间越长，对通胀的影响就越大 ，需要上调利率的可能性就越高 。
• 英国央行进一步推动量化紧缩政策 ，将以年均 460亿英镑 （每年主动出售 200 亿英镑债券以及债券到期不再复购 ）的速度减持 ，计划在2 034年9月前将持有的政府债券存量清零 。
通胀高企导致市场对于英国央行后期加息预期较高 ，市场押注英国央行将在明年 7 月前累计加息 4轮。对此，行长贝利表示委员会这次进行了大量讨论 ，但并未讨论加息四次的

前景，目前情况实在不可预测 。
• 持续加息和缩表令英国国债遭遇越来越高的抛售压力 ，本周30 年英国国债收益率一度冲高至 5.867%，创1998年以来新高 。

### Page 8

欧盟 7 月贸易顺差创年内新高 9 月ZEW 经济指数出现分化
资料来源：Wind、iFinD、 方正 中期 期货 研究 院
• 7月欧盟进出口贸易总额录得 4863 亿欧元，其中进口和出口贸易额分别录得 2391 亿欧元和 2471 亿欧元，贸易顺差达到 80亿欧元，创
今年新高。欧盟贸易顺差主要来源于美国 （173亿欧元 ）、澳大利亚 （30亿欧元）、加拿大 （20亿欧元）。欧盟最大贸易逆差国来
源于中国，7 月扩大至366亿欧元，创2022 年11月以来新高；对韩国和印度逆差分别达到 19亿欧元和 16亿欧元。5月欧盟贸易出现自
2023 年2月以来最大逆差，叠加中东局势紧张 ，引发欧盟高层极度恐慌 ，诸多欧洲政府官员悲观认为欧盟经济正在直面滞涨风险 。7
月贸易顺差扩大 ，可以缓和欧盟高层紧张情绪 ，不过7月顺差增量主要源于对美国顺差扩大 ，这与美加墨世界杯补库和关税抢运有
关。如今世界杯已经结束 ，美国落地多项关税 ，未来仍需关注欧盟能否延续对美国较高顺差 。7月欧盟对中国贸易逆差进一步扩大 ，

可能导致中欧贸易关系进一步僵化 。前不久欧盟将 10月设定为取得中欧谈判切实成果的关键期限 ，欧盟贸易专员谢夫乔维奇威胁 ，
若与中方的谈判在10月中旬前没有实质成果，欧盟将考虑对中国关闭市场 。
• 9月欧元区ZEW经济现状指数录得 -13.9，连续三个月上升 ，创美伊冲突爆发以来新高；景气指数录得 25.8，低于前值31.4，结束了
连续四个月的上升势头 。其中，欧洲经济火车头 ——德国的9月ZEW经济现状和景气指数分别录得 34.7和-47.1，分别创造今年3月和
203 年6月以来新高。德国依靠宽松的财政政策推动基建发展 ，今年的经济发展前景确实大为改善 ，也带动了整个欧元区的经济发展 。
不过，9月中东战局有扩大化和升级化的倾向，布伦特原油价格重返 110美元/桶附近高位 ，对于欧元区经济蒙上一层阴影 。

2026 年 9 月 9 日(水)

1/3 
日銀の利上げ「終了」を先読みするには 
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・市場参加者が予想するターミナルレートの代理指標として、一般的に参照されている２年先
１年金利は2.4％近傍で推移している。ここから判断すると３～４ヶ月に一度の利上げが、２
年程度続くことが想定されているようである。

・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPY は先行き12 ヶ月 155 円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を９月に1.25％とした後、27 年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FED はFF 金利を、年内は3.75％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

2/3 
・他方、エコノミスト等を含む市場関係者の中心的な見通しは、やや控えめである。QUICK月次
調査＜債券＞（調査期間：2026年8月25日～27日）によると「今回の日銀利上げ局面における
政策金利のターミナルレートはいくらと予想しますか」との質問に対して、中央値と最頻値
は1.75％、単純平均値は1.9％であった。「この１カ月でターミナルレートの見通しは変化し
ましたか」との質問に対しては回答者の47％が「引き上げた」とし、53％が「変化なし」と
答えていた。風向きとしてはターミナルレートが上昇する方向にあるものの、あと３回で利
上げが終了するとの見通しが現時点における一つのコンセンサスとなっている。なお、筆者
は2027年末までに2.25％に到達するとみており、ターミナルレートは2.25％～2.5％を想定し
ている。

・利上げが比較的早期に終了するとしたら、そのきっかけや理由は何であろうか。もちろん最
重要変数は物価である。ただし消費者物価指数は、ガソリンの旧暫定税率廃止、電気・ガス
代の補助、教育関係費の無償化、そして消費税減税といった政策要因に攪乱されるため、実
勢を把握することは困難であり、金融政策を読む上で「使えない指標」に成り下がってしま
っている。

・ところで物価は遅行指標である。過去の経済活動の結果が時差を伴って物価に反映されてい
く。したがって、金融政策を運営する側にとっては、既往の利上げが現在の経済活動にどう
いった影響を与えているのかを計測する必要がある。そうした意味において速報性に優れて
いるのは日銀短観の企業金融項目であろう。企業が感じる金融環境は、設備投資などの意思
決定に無視できない影響を与えるほか、資金繰りが窮屈になれば倒産が増える事態にも発展
（出所）QUICK 月次調査＜債券＞

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

する。

・日銀短観６月調査（全規模・全産業）において貸出態度判断ＤＩ（緩い－厳しい）は＋13、
資金繰り判断ＤＩ（楽である-厳しい）は＋11と、双方とも長期的にみて良好な水準にあり、
非線形な動きは観察されていない。現時点の評価としては、2024年３月以降の累積的な利上
げにもかかわらず、金融環境はほとんど引き締まっていない。もっとも、今後、累積的な利
上げの効果がこれら指標に表面化してくる可能性はあり、仮に非線形な悪化を示した場合、
政策委員の間では経済・物価のオーバーキル、すなわち過剰な引き締めに対する警戒感が共
有されるだろう。利上げ終了を早期に判断する指標として、これらが有効であると筆者は考
える。

Document 611: 日銀は1.25％へ政策金利を引き上げ ２つの反対票について考える

Source PDF: all report pdf/日銀は1.25％へ政策金利を引き上げ ２つの反対票について考える.pdf

### Page 1

2026 年 9 月 18 日(金)

1/2 
日銀は 1.25％へ政策金利を引き上げ ２つの反対票について考える 
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・浅田委員と佐藤委員が反対票を投じた理由は概ね同様であり、双方とも経済・物価の基調が
それほど強くないなかで、利上げは適切でないとの主張であった。この点はややハト派な印
象を与えたかもしれない。

・現時点で、今後の利上げに際して慎重姿勢を示しそうな委員は、この２名のみである。もっ
とも、2027年７月23日の高田委員と田村委員の任期満了を見据えると、この２名が反対票を
投じ続けることには一定の意味がある。というのも、高市政権が後任にハト派（≒リフレ
派）委員を２名送り込むことで、４票の反対票が投じられる、或いは政策委員会の分裂を避
けるために利上げ提案そのものが引っ込められる、との見方があるからだ。

・この点、筆者は2027年７月の審議委員人事は、一部の市場参加者の予想・懸念に反して、中
立的ないしはタカ派的な人選になるとみている。というのも、現在の政策委員会で最もタカ
派的な２名の後任人事が、一転してリフレ色の強い人選となれば、それは政府による露骨な
・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPY は先行き12 ヶ月 155 円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を12 月に 1.5％とした後、27 年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FED はFF 金利を、年内は4.0％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
日銀支配に映ってしまいかねない。この初夏に経験した骨太ショックと呼ばれる円安・長期
金利上昇は、日銀の独立性が脅かされるとの懸念が一部の市場参加者で生じたことが原因で
あったと筆者は理解している。政権側がそれに対して迅速に軌道修正したことに鑑みると、
同じようなリスクは取らないと考えるのが自然であろう。2027年７月より先は政策委員の構
成変化によって利上げが停止されるとの見方があるが、筆者はその可能性は限定的であると
みている。

・仮に2027年７月の人事にハト派色の強い人物を充ててしまうと、その頃に話題となっている
はずの次期総裁・副総裁人事（2028年３・４月）も同じような人選になることを市場参加者
が想定し、円安・長期金利上昇が進行する恐れが高まる。そうしたリスクを取ってまでも、
政策金利を低く抑えようとするだろうか。

Document 616: 春江水暖鸭先知！华尔街“预警”：“交易狂潮”正失去动力

Source PDF: all report pdf/春江水暖鸭先知！华尔街“预警”：“交易狂潮”正失去动力.pdf
Report source: Unknown

### Page 1

华尔街交易盛宴落幕？美国银⾏ CEO ⼀句 " 持平 " 令股价单⽇暴跌 5% ，并拖累同业集体⾛
低。继 Q2 四⼤⾏股票交易收⼊合计飙升 72% 创历史纪录后，⾼管们罕⻅集体下调三季度前
瞻指引， AI 热潮、亚洲半导体投机与 SpaceX 巨型 IPO 共振制造的 " 异常季度 " 难以复制。
摘要：
华尔街⼤⾏⾼管集体发出警⽰信号：推动第⼆季度利润创纪录的交易热潮正在降温，市场业务
的⾼增⻓势头难以为继。
本周，美国各⼤银⾏⾼管在⼀场⾏业会议上相继下调前瞻指引。美国银⾏⾸席执⾏官 Brian
Moynihan 率先表态，预计该⾏第三季度销售与交易收⼊将 " 持平 " ，消息⼀出，美国银⾏股价当
⽇重挫 5% ，并拖累同业股价⾛低。摩根⼤通和花旗集团虽给出相对乐观的预测，但与第⼆季度
的爆发式增⻓相⽐，增速已明显放缓。
这⼀系列表态标志着华尔街交易业务的景⽓周期出现拐点，对于押注银⾏股持续受益于市场波
动的投资者⽽⾔，需重新审视相关仓位。
第⼆季度：⼀个 " 异常出⾊ " 的季度
今年第⼆季度，华尔街交易业务创下历史性佳绩。摩根⼤通、⾼盛、花旗和美国银⾏四家机构

的股票交易收⼊合计同⽐飙升 72% ，达到 193 亿美元。
这场盛宴的背后，是多重因素共振：投资者对⼈⼯智能相关股票的狂热追捧、亚洲半导体板块
的投机浪潮，以及 SpaceX 超⼤规模 IPO 带动的股市剧烈波动，共同催⽣了罕⻅的交易活跃度。
摩根⼠丹利联席总裁 Daniel Simkowitz 对此直⾔不讳： “ 今年第⼆季度的市场表现相当出⾊，可
以说，第三季度不会是第⼆季度的翻版。 ”
第三季度：增速分化，但普遍降档
尽管各⾏对第三季度的展望存在分歧，但增速放缓已是共识。
摩根⼤通联席总裁 Doug Petno 预计，在经历创纪录的季度之后，市场业务收⼊将出现 " 季节性环
⽐下滑 " ，但该⾏仍指引第三季度交易收⼊同⽐增⻓ " ⼗⼏个百分点 " 。花旗集团则预测其市场部
⻔将实现中个位数百分⽐的增⻓。
相⽐之下，美国银⾏的 " 持平 " 预期最为保守。 Brian Moynihan 指出，亚洲融资活动已明显降温，
该地区的主经纪业务 —— 即向对冲基⾦、交易公司及家族办公室提供的借贷服务 —— 出现明显

回落。
⾼盛⾸席执⾏官 David Solomon 则表⽰，该⾏股票业务 " 依然⾮常强劲 " ，但固定收益、货币及⼤
宗商品业务收⼊将 " 略有⾛软 " 。过去⼀周，⾼盛股价已累计下跌约 7% 。
结构性需求犹存，⻓期逻辑未变
尽管短期增速承压，华尔街⾼管普遍强调市场业务的⻓期结构性机遇并未消退。

摩根⼤通的 Petno 着重指出，主经纪业务和结构性融资需求 " 显著增加 " ，并认为这⼀趋势 " 将持续
相当⻓时间 " 。

将以某种速度复合增⻓，那么我们为客⼾提供融资的空间也将以相应速度复合增⻓。 " 他同时承
认，增⻓不会是⼀条直线。
华尔街各⼤⾏将于下⽉陆续公布截⾄ 9 ⽉底的第三季度财报，届时交易业务的实际表现将得到检
验。

Document 617: 昨天发布会的“三个字”和“一句回答”--整个华尔街都在琢磨“沃什的路数”

Source PDF: all report pdf/昨天发布会的“三个字”和“一句回答”--整个华尔街都在琢磨“沃什的路数”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-19
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

在沃什的框架⾥，⽬标区间升⾄ 3.75%-4% 之后，政策⽴场依然具有刺激性 —— 加息只是撤
回了其中 " ⼀剂 " 。⽽中性利率 —— 过去⼗多年美联储判断政策松紧的核⼼锚 —— 被从操作层
⾯搁置。有分析指出，沃什正在将美联储的决策逻辑向货币主义⽅向牵引，称之为⼀次 " 地
震级转向 " 。
摘要：
昨天加息后的新闻发布会上，美联储主席沃什⽤三个字撤回 "dose of accommodation" （⼀剂宽
松）定义了本次加息的性质，被追问利率相对于中性利率的位置时，他的回应同样耐⼈寻味：
这⼀概念 " 在学术上有⽤ " ，但 " 对我们今天的决策没有任何操作层⾯的效果 " 。
三个词加⼀句回答，正在迫使华尔街重新理解这届美联储的加息路径和政策框架。
在沃什的框架⾥，⽬标区间升⾄ 3.75%-4% 之后，政策⽴场依然具有刺激性 —— 加息只是撤回了
其中 " ⼀剂 " 。⽽中性利率 —— 过去⼗多年美联储判断政策松紧的核⼼锚 —— 被从操作层⾯搁置。
CME FedWatch 数据显⽰， 10 ⽉再次加息的概率已从⼀周前的 42% 跳升⾄约 58% 。⾼盛和美银

先后追加加息预期，美银还预计 12 ⽉再加⼀次。期货隐含的 2027 年底利率为 4.635% ，指向还有
三到四次加息。
"Dose of Accommodation" ：加息只是移除了⼀剂宽松
沃什在发布会上多次重复这⼀表述，并指出做出加息决定的基础是美国经济看起来已经 " ⾛
强 " （ strengthened ），且⾦融条件变得不那么紧缩。
Evercore ISI 经济学与央⾏策略主管 Krishna Guha 在客⼾报告中称，这是发布会 " 最突出的鹰派
元素 " 。 " 这不是⼝误。他多次重复，显然经过深思熟虑， "Guha 写道， " 这种框架与美联储近年来
使⽤的表述有实质性区别，暗⽰加息次数可能是开放式的。 "
BNP Paribas Securities ⾸席美国经济学家 James Egelhof 的解读称：在美联储的辞典⾥，
accommodation 就是 stimulus （刺激）。 " 这意味着当前货币政策⽴场具有显著的刺激性。在政策
起点具有刺激性、周期性动⼒强劲、通胀持续的条件下，可能需要⼤幅加息 —— 或许超过我们

预期的三次 —— 才能稳定失业率、防⽌明年经济过热。 "
如果按字⾯意思理解沃什的框架 ——Guha 进⼀步指出 ——" 利率可能必须持续上升，直到私⼈部
⻔⾯临的⾦融条件不再是 ' 宽松的 '—— ⽆论如何定义。这是⼀个相当开放式的前景。 "
中性利率出局，货币主义登场
发布会上， CNBC 记者 Steve Liesman 追问当前利率距中性利率（ r* ）有多远。
沃什说，他作为经济学学⽣研究过中性利率，即以瑞典经济学家维克塞尔命名的 "Wicksellian
real rate" 。这个概念 " 在学术上有⽤，是帮助我们思考政策的讨论 " ，但 " 对我们今天做出的决策
没有任何操作层⾯的效果 " 。
这句话需要放在背景⾥理解。⾃伯南克时代以来，中性利率⼀直是美联储制定政策的核⼼参照
系 —— 利率⾼于中性⽔平即为紧缩，低于即为宽松，政策讨论围绕这个看不⻅的基准展开。沃
什将它定性为纯学术讨论，相当于拆掉了这套运⾏⼗余年的定位系统。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

写评论
图⽚
有 分 析 指 出 ， 沃 什 正 在 将 美 联 储 的 决 策 逻 辑 向 货 币 主 义⽅ 向 牵 引 ， 称 之 为 ⼀ 次 " 地 震 级 转
向 " （ seismic shift ）。他的前任鲍威尔 —— 现以理事⾝份留在 FOMC—— 曾多次明确拒绝货币主
义的基本原则。⽽沃什早在 8 ⽉杰克逊霍尔会议上就已亮出⽅向，提出货币供应量变化与经济活
动及通胀之间存在关联。
本次发布会进⼀步确认了这⼀取向。沃什阐明了⼏项与货币主义⾼度⼀致的⽴场：⻝品、能源
等个别价格变动不 " 导致 " 通胀，美联储要确保这些相对价格变化不产⽣⼆阶、三阶效应；单个数
据点 " 充满噪声 " ，重要的是趋势；美联储处理总量问题 —— 劳动⼒市场、 GDP 、总⽀出和整体通

胀，但他同时承认，没有⾦融资产、靠薪⽔⽣活的最低收⼊群体将从物价稳定中获益最多。
货币供应量的数据已经提供了线索。沃什在发布会上⾄少两次表⽰，近⼏个⽉他很难将⾦融条
件描述为 " 紧缩的 " 。分析指出，过去 6 ⾄ 9 个⽉美国⼴义货币供应量增速维持在 6%-8% 区间，明
显过⾼ —— 要实现 2% 的通胀⽬标，这⼀增速需降⾄ 6% 左右。相⽐抽象且不可观测的中性利率，
货币供应增速提供了更直接的政策判断依据。
华尔街在定价什么
Natixis Investment Managers ⾸ 席 组 合 策 略 师 Jack Janasiewicz 认 为 ， "dose of
accommodation" 的表述 " 强化了鹰派基调，暗⽰委员会不再认为政策是适度紧缩的 " ，但他 " 不相
信这是⼀个激进新紧缩周期的开始 " ，⽽更倾向于理解为 " 撤回美联储在 2025 年秋季实施的保险性
降息 " 。
如果期货定价兑现，沃什的美联储⾄少将逆转前任鲍威尔在任时批准的多数降息。但当下分歧

的焦点已超越加息次数本⾝ —— 当中性利率退出操作层⾯、货币供应量⾛上前台，市场需要适
应的是⼀整套新的政策坐标体系。三场发布会之后，沃什给出了他的框架轮廓，但加息的终点
在哪⾥，他没有给出答案。

 上週美國市場概況：
上週美股在輝達 Q2 財報營收超預期年增 106% 下領軍科技類股成為領漲產業，同時 Salesforce
與 CrowdStrike 的優異財報帶動軟體族群走高。因此資金從防禦價值類股出走，醫療、地產等領
跌族群。另外數據方面在略超預期的 7 月 PCE 以及市場對華許在 Jackson Hole 年會的鷹派解讀，
持續堅定 2% 通膨的長期目標，使9月升息預期再進一步走升至 57%，帶動殖利率曲線熊市平坦
化的趨勢，整體同步使美元美債殖利率再度走強 ，使重資產、利率敏感型類股普跌 ，黃金現貨盤
中跳水當日也走跌 3.22%。然而市場廣度近期雖持續 走降，但仍提供大盤一定支撐，最終三大指

數整週小幅下跌，標普 500 下跌 0.25% 至 7711.76，納斯達克指數下跌 0.52% 至 26402.42，道
瓊工業指下跌 0.02% 至 53559.99。另外雖 WTI 原油、布蘭特原油在週五收盤分別在 82.76 及
87.97，但週末美軍再度空襲 了荷姆茲 海峽拉拉克島，伊朗飛彈與無人機報復約旦及阿拉伯聯合
大公國的美軍基地，油價再度面臨潛在的走高壓力。
 本週重點關注：
本週關注重點看法將圍繞在聯準 會貨幣政策轉向，以及中東地緣政治引發的通膨隱憂 。隨著市場
進入歷史表現最弱的 「9月魔咒」季節，短期內市場 波動可能將 明顯放大 。數據方面，雖第二季
GDP 修正值顯民間內需 (C+I) 在持續上修，然而短期經濟看法仍須留意 ，8月ISM採購經理人報
告以及8月非農報告；美股財 報留意博通(AVGO)、戴爾 (DELL) 、帕羅奧圖網路公司 (PANW)、
MongoDB(MDB)、Snowflake (SNOW)。

 市場正在持續關注大型科技類股資本支出及其營收的效率 ，科技雖仍是未來股市上漲的主要動能，不宜看空已錯過行情，但資金仍有部分流往防
禦價值等類股，同時新任聯準會主席華許的上任，其在 Jackson Hole 的鷹派結果，使美國公債殖利率仍有走高之壓力，同步美國欲日本債務的隱
憂持續共振全球債市的走高。 9 月 FOMC 將是關鍵。因此建議此時適度佈局具備防禦性質之醫療、核心消費族群。雖股市未明顯走跌仍在高檔震

盪，但此時可同步降低成長、循環型類股部位抑或是降低買入力道，以作為提前預防風險的到來。
 非美市場方面，歐洲近期各國的通膨進一步走高，市場升息預期存續；日本由於近期因實施電費與燃氣等補貼，然而薪資通膨以及日圓貶勢未反
轉下，通膨風險仍大，日圓在 9 月初仍跌回 160 元的大關，10 年期國債殖利率將突破 3%，同步升息預期仍有強勁的預期，總體而言日、歐股市
在短線上謹慎中性看待，長線仍看好。債券方面，目前公司債信用利差走高有限，代表信用風險仍然可控，適合長期鎖利率布局投資等級公司債 ；
採取中長天期搭配短天期的配置。總結以上，第一，需慎選財報優異且敘事穩定的族群；第二 ，適度配置防禦型類股，並且配合短期敘事的優勢
類股；第三，布局投資等級債來進行長期配置。

高資產財富顧問中心
 勞動市場疲軟中見改善步伐
7 月堪薩斯城聯儲勞動市場狀況指數走升，顯示就業環境改善，企業招聘相對有意願回升、失業率維持低檔至4.1%，勞動市場動能相對近期增強。
雖7月非農就業淨減少2.3萬人，但很大一部分來自公部門的5.3萬的勞動人口流出。整體來看，勞動市場狀況仍處於較疲軟的位階，但指數上揚代表
勞動需求改善，有助於延續經濟動能。
 實質消費支出動能停滯，然可支配所得仍持續成長
7 月實質個人消費支出月增率近乎停滯，反映民眾支出趨於謹慎，儲蓄率由2.7%升至3%，但仍有部分反映在 prime day 等折扣期間的提前消費，另
外第二季GDP 修正值維持不變在1.5%的季增年率，但C+I的整體內需指標季增年率由原先的4.0%升至4.2%，其中的消費由3.2%升至3.4%的；同時

7 月可支配所得仍維持成長。整體看來，消費動能未出現明顯惡化跡象，但仍須留意走弱的跡象。
 整體來看經濟未明顯強勁復甦，但仍在弱勢中保有韌性
6
7 月的勞動市場與消費數據未完全走弱，經濟保有韌性美國專欄

美國零售汽車用普通汽油平均即期價格 WTI近月期貨價格(USD/Gallon) (USD/barrel)(%)
 通膨與能源價格
美國三大核心通膨指標（CPI、PCE、PPI）自 2022 年高峰後持續回落，顯示物價壓力已大幅緩解。然而截至現在，聯準會目前看重的 PCE 物價年
增來到 3.7%，仍高於市場預期3.6%，並且即便是核心項目年增率也高於聯準會，同時也是華許上任以來所堅持的 2% 的長期目標，政策端短期內
仍具有進一步限制性的壓力。
 油價預計維持震盪，觀察油價走勢
WTI 近月期貨在 8 月小幅反彈，油價回升至每桶約 80 美元以上，美國零售汽油均價亦同步微升至 4.0 美元/加侖。雖然能源回升的漲幅有限，但在

美伊和談進展仍顛簸、且美方預期將對伊朗祭出強力制裁的背景下，油價可能維持當前水準，若在事態進一步發展下，並可能高於 7 月平均，對短
期通膨將會形成支撐，持續觀察。
 整體來看，通膨雖如預期的走降，但油價與政策不確定的存續讓鷹派情緒在市場上
資料來源：Bloomberg、聯邦整理

高資產財富顧問中心
8
Jackson Hole 年會華許強調2%通膨目標，引爆升息預期美國專欄
資料來源：Bloomberg、FED、聯邦整理
 政策溝通轉型，淡化前瞻性指引：
前瞻性指引等危機時期的工具在常態下已過時，過度承諾未來路徑會引發「鏡像
效應」並限制政策彈性，同時政策步調以『Kohn Day』的快速死亡行軍，還是
『Bernanke Day』的悠閒步調，來類比政策未來不是僵化的唯一方向 ，暗示將
是目前環境當下做出彈性改變，同時華許希望以藉由工作小組進行預測且同時具
經驗證的方式來表達反映函數，避免2021年因前瞻指引錯誤而誤判通膨路徑。
 通膨仍高於 2% 目標，當前更偏向通膨應對：
PCE 年增率達 3.7%、6個月變動率達 4.1%，且199個子成分項目漲幅仍高於 3%，

同時大宗商品價格仍持續上漲；雖中期通膨 Swap 市場大致穩定，但短期在通膨
未明確且足夠速度回落至 2% 前，首要焦點依然是穩定物價。
 經濟與就業展現韌性，金融環境偏寬鬆：
受 AI 基礎設施投資拉動，企業資本支出與標普500獲利增長強勁；失業率維持在
4.1% 的歷史低檔 ，勞動市場符合充分就業；整體 信用利差處於低位，顯示廣泛
金融環境並未受實質壓制。
 AI 為新生產要素，持續探討其影響：
AI 可能為為新生產要素 ，其普及究竟能否持續提升 生產力，對經濟與政策又如
何產生影響、如何改變資本與勞動的互動、資本回報將落在何處、在何種時間尺
度上、稀缺資產的擁有者剩餘價值將如何分配、Token 價格是否會出現異質性，
這些問題仍未有定論。將持續由工作小組進行深入探討。
 整體來看，華許在年會上持續強調更新框架與作法，堅守通膨 2% 目標，但未提供
前瞻指引。與 7 月 FOMC 相比，當時更著重於等待夏季通膨數據再評估，維持利
率不變，卻因缺乏額外資訊使市場擔憂其通膨管理 ，「我們不知道華許怎麼做？」

市場對長期的額外風險貼水增生，當日收盤 30 年期殖利率上升 1.2bps，2 年期卻
下滑 2.6bps。到了 8 月年會，市場理解華許的框架在於政策彈性，態度傾向市場
需從實體經濟自行找尋答案，其也從信用利差、勞動市場、金融條件、AI 投資及美
股獲利等面向，側面指出勞動市場非首要之務，讓市場認為通膨才是後續重點，雖
未透露 9 月FOMC的舉措，但提升了「高利率更久」甚至再升息的「可能」的市場
認知。從年會當天2年期公債仍走高 4.2bps 可以看出這樣的傾向。整體而言，本次
年會華許讓市場從霧裡看花抓到了些許分析解讀的空間 ，自行描摹可能的政策方向。
 另外一個原因在於，當前背景下的通膨壓力更巨大，先前 6 月 FOMC 正處和解備
忘錄的調停，停戰在即，油價大幅走降提振市場信心； 7 月則面臨 6 月 PCE 年增
大幅走降，雖美伊再度和談失敗 ，但仍釋放外交緩和訊號 。反觀8月的油價高檔震
盪，雙方打擊再起短期無進一步和談的意願，讓擔憂加深，8月CPI的壓力更加巨大。

市場解讀華許放鷹，推升 9 月升息預期，整週曲線牛市平坦化美債專欄
美債殖利率曲線高檔震盪，中天期變動較大
資料來源：Bloomberg、聯邦整理
(%)
 債市回顧 ：上週原先在能源價格走 低下，債市一度延續漲勢帶動殖利率下行； 然而 7 月PCE物價指數年增略高預期 ，隨後週五市場盤中聯 準會主席華許在
Jackson Hole 年會上強調通膨仍高於 2% 目標且物價穩定為首要考量，促使市場對 9 月升息 1 碼的預期大幅跳升至 57%，殖利率曲線被帶動呈熊平傾向(殖利
率曲線整體上移)，然而整週仍呈現牛市平坦化(長天期殖利率下跌、短天期殖利率上升)的結果。2 年期美債殖利率週五單日拉升 12 個基點收於 4.34%，全週累
計上揚 11 個基點，而 10 年期美債殖利率雖升至 4.72%，全週仍微跌 2 個基點。
 短期展望：華許在的 Jackson Hole 年會所引發的升息壓力以及週末美伊再度開打的消息，將使美債的壓力將直至聯準會 9 月 FOMC 表態之前。後續若美伊情

勢未讓油價顯著走低，整體公債的壓力仍然會存在，圖轉為鷹派的氛圍預計使殖利率曲線呈高檔震盪，偏走高的格局。後續觀察要點，總經部分需留意本週的8
月非農就業報告，以及 8 月 ISM 採購經理人報告。

NVDA 輝達營收遠超預期，並給出超預期營收指引
資料來源：Bloomberg、輝達、聯邦整理

輝達自由現金流仍有 200 億美元水準，但相較以往大減
 輝達營收超出預期 40 億美元，稀釋後 EPS 亦超預期 25 %
輝達 (NVDA) 本季營收年增 106% 至 962.2 億美元，遠超市場預期的 921 億美元；毛利率增加 2.5% 至 75%； 稀釋後 EPS 年增 128% 至 2.46 美
元。，亦遠超市場預期的 2.06 美元。自由現金流年增 58.7% 至 213.4 億美元。本季資料中心營收年增 117% 至 890 億美元。
輝達下季營收預計突破千億美元大關，毛利率略降
(十億美元) (十億美元)
 除業績超預期外，下季指引亦大超預期
輝達罕見給出明確的下季營收指引，公司預期下一季營收將達 1080 億美元 (並不包含中國市場)，但受記憶體漲價影響，毛利率略降為 73% ~ 74%。
CEO 黃仁勳表示，AI 已從單純的實驗性訓練跨入實質創造營收與生產力的階段，因此客戶端對即時推論與 Token 產出的商業回報顯著提升，驅動
各大雲端巨頭與企業持續追加算力資本支出。並且新一代架構與硬體 Vera Rubin 平台開始進入全面量產階段，向 CoreWeave (CRWV)、Google

Cloud (GOOGL)、Microsoft Azure (MSFT)、Oracle Cloud Infrastructure (ORCL) 等雲端夥伴交付。

NVDA 業績雖不斷超預期，但市場擔心的風險亦逐步增加

資料來源：Bloomberg、輝達、聯邦整理
輝達價值創造利差仍在極高區間，但逐漸縮小仍會影響估值
 輝達應收帳款增加速度明顯快過營收成長幅度，很可能是放大市場擔憂一大主因
本季，輝達營收年增率為 962.2 億美元，年增率為 106%，季增率為 18%，而本季應收帳款額度為 630.6 億美元，季增率高達 55%，年增率亦高達
218%，兩者皆顯著高於營收的成長速度 ，顯示輝達雖然業務成長快速 ，但收款速度卻遠不如應有水準 。雖然目前其總負債比率僅 28.5%、計息債
務比例 10.42%，而淨債務 (計息總負債減現金與高流動性資產) 為「-232.2 億美元」，意為輝達債務上實際仍處於正向創造現金的水準，本身債務
體質仍穩健。
然而，輝達在業務擴張的同時，應收帳款的比例卻越來越高，本季應收帳款比例已占整體營收近 66%，應收帳款周轉天數也由去年同期的 33 天大
幅增加至上一季的 44.3 天與本季的 48.2 天，持續增加的趨勢明顯。代表除客戶未付款的比例增加，客戶還錢的速度也下降。另外，在價值創造利

差 (ROIC - WACC) 方面，由去年同期的 88.65% 收斂至上一季的 74.9%，再到本季的 69.96%。對於許多機構而言可能是估值修正的原因之一。雖
然輝達產業基本面仍相當穩固，此點也證實在其營收指引與巨頭資本支出中，但財務流動穩定性的隱憂可能壓抑市場對其基本面的情緒。
輝達應收帳款佔營收比例增加，且自身與客戶皆大量發債，引發憂慮
(十億美元) (%)

美國股市 資本支出成長延續，明年可能達1.3兆美元，記憶體支出佔最多
資料來源：TrendForce、聯邦整理
 本季雲巨頭財報上調資本支出至總和 8300 億美元，多家研究機構預估資本支出指引將持續增加至明年
本季財報北美四大 CSP 資本支出再度調升，微軟 (MSFT) 支出展望升至 1900 億美元，年增近 130%，這當中約 250 億美元為應對價格上漲；
Google (GOOGL) 資本支出指引由 1750 ~ 1850 億美元上調至 1800 ~ 1900 億美元，年增超 100%；Meta (META) 將資本支出預期由 1150 ~
1350 億美元上修至 1250 ~ 1450 億美元，年增率約 85%；亞馬遜 (AMZN) AWS 也將今年資本支出指引調升至約 2300 億美元，年增 50% 。總和

約 7400 億美元。若加入甲骨文 (ORCL) 的 700 ~ 950 億美元指引，今年北美五大 CSP 資本支出總和將達 8300 億美元規模。再涵蓋特斯拉 (TSLA)
的 250 億美元與 SpaceX (SPCX) 預估約 580 億美元的全年資本支出指引，僅美國巨頭的 AI 資本支出就將達到 9000 億美元規模。也是支撐輝達、
美光、超微等持續巨幅成長的主因。而若僅討論北美與中國的九大 CSP 支出，其總規模仍估將破 9000 億美元 (左下圖)，其中北美五大巨頭佔九成。
TrendForce預估九大 CSP 資本支出將持續上調，明年將達 1.3 兆美元
 除輝達、超微等企業外，機構預估明年受惠最多者為記憶體廠商
根據 TrendForce，2027 年度硬體資本支出將有近 68% 適用於記憶體，已超過 GPU 的花費。美光 (MU)、SK 海力士 (SKHY)、三星獲利獲得支撐。

Financial Market 
▎美股大選前震盪，待選後較有表現 
截至 8/27，標普 500 指數年初以來上漲 12.7%，費城
半導體指數雖在 6、7 月出現較明顯修正，但年至今報
酬依然達 65.7%，整體而言美國股市仍偏多頭。不過，
今年 11/3 美國將展開期中選舉，參考過去 1990 年以
來 9 次期中選舉年度，該年標普 500 指數下半年最低
點有 5 次發生在 10 月，且以平均走勢觀察，因反應政
治不確定性，選舉年的 9 月至 10 月中旬，美股有較明
顯修正空間(圖 1)。

圖 1 選舉年股市低點平均落在 10 月上旬

資料來源：Bloomberg, 2026/8/26

展望明年，統計 1946 年以來共 20 次美國期中選舉，
選後隔 1 年僅有 2011 年、2015 年收跌，其餘年度全
數收漲，平均年報酬則為 15.0%(圖 2)。選後高機率收
漲主要原因來自於選舉後政治不確定性降溫 ， 加上敗選
方在選後推出的政策 ，將傾向調整至符合市場及選民的
偏好，特別是一般人民較有感的經濟政策，以備戰下一
次的總統大選，多重因素疊加下，導致美國股市在選後
普遍上漲。

而全年度標普 500 指數漲跌幅則可觀察該年度第 1 季
市場表現 ， 因機構法人第1 季的佈局大致反映其對於全
年股票市場看法，統計 1990 年以來，在扣除 2020 年
疫情影響後 ， 若該年度上漲、 且漲幅高於20%， 即傳統
定義上的牛市，則第 1 季平均漲幅為 7.5%，且上漲機
率為 83.3％，若該年度收漲但漲幅低於 20%，第 1 季
平均漲幅為 1.7%、仍為正報酬，不過上漲機率降至
61.5%，反之，若該年度為熊市、即跌幅大於20%，第
1 季平均漲幅為負 6.1%，下跌機率則為 100%， 若全年
度下跌但跌幅低於 20%，第1 季平均漲跌幅為負 2.0%，
下跌機率為 71.4%(圖 3)，因此，第 1 季表現大致可推
敲該年度美國股市全年方向。

◼ 歷史經驗顯示，美國期中選舉年的 9 月至 10 月中旬受政治不確定性影響，美股
易出現明顯修正。然而選後隨不確定性消退及政策轉向挺經濟，隔年上漲機率極
高。此外，第 1 季股市表現與全年度走勢高度連動，若首季開出紅盤，當年度 美
國股市收漲的機率與表現皆顯著提升。  
◼ 受高基期、電力與土地限制影響，美國 5 大 CPS 資本支出年增率 2027 年預期下
滑。將使半導體等上游供應鏈獲利成長面臨挑戰，進而限縮評價面擴張。由於缺
乏全新題材帶動，市場焦點將從盲目擴展算力轉向關注企業的 AI 實質變現能力。  
◼ 受通膨與財政赤字隱憂影響， 聯準會明年上半年要啟動降息門檻相對較高 ，高利
率環境將持續壓抑高估值 類股。在此背景下，資金預期將流向評價面相對便宜的
板塊 ， 建議2027 年除泛科技股外，亦可側重非必需消費 、 原物料及能源等具備性
價比優勢的類股。

自 ChatGPT 問市以來，因算力需求光速成長，美國雲

器上游企業 ， 明年獲利增速要維持高成長較具挑戰空間 。

▎27H1 維持緊縮，關注低估值類股  
今年下半年標普 500 指數中的資訊科技類股表現平淡 ，
6/30 以來僅小漲 0.02%，表現略低於同期間標普 500
指數約 2.1%漲幅，且整體而言大盤下半年表現遠落後
於上半年。部分原因為美伊戰爭以來，美國物價壓力逼
迫聯準會維持偏鷹派立場，在 沃許(Warsh)上任聯準會
主席後，市場利率並未如川普所預期的下降，美國 10
年期公債殖利率甚至一度突破 4.7%，造成評價面較高
的類股如資訊科技等，市場追價意願不高。展望明年，
預估在油價帶動下，美國上半年 PCE、核心 PCE 增速
仍會使聯準會難以有降息動作， 彭博預估2027 年 Q1、
Q2 核心 PCE 年增率分別仍有 2.7%、2.5%，較聯準會
2%目標仍有一段距離 ，加上市場縈繞對美國財政赤字
的擔憂，上半年美債殖利率或延續高位整理，將不利估
值較高的類股。 統計標普500 指數與 11 大類股預估本
益比，房地產、非必需消費、原物料、通訊服務、公用
事業、能源，預估本益比相對大盤的比值，較其他類股
來的更低，在比價效應之下上述類股或可吸引資金進駐 ，
惟考量房地產及公用事業對於利率敏感度更高 ， 因此可
更側重在非必需消費、原物料、通訊服務及能源股，或
增持價值型類股。

Document 744: 長期金利3％、家計は30年前より強くなったのか ～金融資産はほぼ倍増、家計ごとに異なる「金利耐性」～

Source PDF: all report pdf/長期金利3％、家計は30年前より強くなったのか ～金融資産はほぼ倍増、家計ごとに異なる「金利耐性」～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-09-09
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Flash Insights

2026 年 9 月 9 日

1/4 
長期金利 3％、家計は 30 年前より強くなったのか 
～金融資産はほぼ倍増、家計ごとに異なる「金利耐性」～ 
 政策調査部 フェロー  谷口 智明

1．30 年ぶりの長期金利 3％が示すもの 
2026 年9 月 1 日の国内債券市場で、長期金利の代表的な指標である新発10 年国債利回りが3％
台に上昇した。3％台は1996 年 9 月以来、約30 年ぶりである。翌2 日には一時3.015％まで上昇
し、約 30 年ぶりの水準を更新した。背景には、海外金利の上昇や原油高を受けたインフレ懸念、
日本銀行の追加利上げ観測、財政拡張への警戒など、複数の要因があるとみられる。 
30 年ぶりと聞けば、日本の金利が30 年前に戻ったようにもみえる。だが、1996 年と現在で
は、金利を取り巻く経済環境は大きく異なる。当時は、景気回復を支えるため金融緩和が続き、
長期金利も年度後半には大きく低下した。日本経済はそこからデフレと一段の低金利へ向かい、
ゼロ金利政策や量的緩和、さらにはマイナス金利政策へと進んでいく。 
現在は逆に、長く続いた超低金利から離れていく過程にある。内閣府「令和8 年度年次経済財
政報告」 （以下、 「経済財政白書2026」）が指摘するように、日本経済は、賃金・物価の変動が乏し
かった「低温経済」から、賃金・物価とともに金利も動く経済へ移行しつつある。1996 年の3％
が低金利へ向かう過程の3％だったとすれば、現在の3％は超低金利から抜け出す過程の3％とい
える。同じ3％でも、その方向と意味は異なる。 
本稿では、約30 年前と現在の家計の金融資産・負債の変化を整理したうえで、経済財政白書
2026 の試算を手がかりに、金利上昇の影響が世代・世帯によってどのように異なるのかをみてい
く。ここでいう家計の「強さ」とは、主として金融資産・負債の構成からみた金利上昇への財務
的な耐性を指す。

2．金融資産はほぼ倍増、住宅ローンの比重は上昇 
金利を受け止める家計の姿も、30 年前とは大きく変わった。日本銀行「資金循環統計」に基づ
き、1995 年度以降の家計の金融資産・負債（注1）と、家計負債に占める住宅貸付の割合をみて
みよう（資料1）。

### Page 2

2/4 
資料 1 家計の金融資産・負債と住宅貸付比率の推移 
（注）2004 年度末以前の計数は旧基準のデータが接続されており、現行基準とは一部不連続がある。 
（出所）日本銀行「資金循環統計」より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

金融資産は1996 年度末の約1,260 兆円から、2025 年度末には約2,386 兆円へとほぼ倍増した。
これに対し、負債は概ね400 兆円前後で推移し、2025 年度末も約414 兆円である。マクロでみた
家計部門の金融資産超過幅は30 年前より大きく拡大したことになる。金融資産残高が増えた分、
預金や債券等から得られる利子収入が増加する余地も30 年前より大きくなった。ただし、ここで
みているのは名目残高による家計部門全体の比較であり、一世帯当たりや実質ベース等でみた家
計の余裕まで、同様に拡大したとは限らない。また、金融資産の構成をみると、株式や投資信託
の比重が高まっており、金利上昇が利子所得に及ぼす効果は家計の資産構成によって異なる。 
負債総額は大きく変わっていないが、その構成は変化している。負債には、住宅貸付（住宅ロ
ーン）のほか、消費者信用（消費者金融や教育ローン等）や個人事業主の借入などが含まれる。
家計負債に占める住宅ローンの割合は、1996 年度末の約4 割から上昇し、2010 年代以降は概ね6
割程度で推移している。2025 年度末の住宅ローン残高は約 244 兆円と、1996 年度末の約162 兆円
から約 82 兆円増加した。この 30 年間で金融資産が大幅に積み上がる一方、負債側では住宅ロー
ンの存在感が高まったことになる。重要なのは、家計部門全体のバランスシートが厚みを増して
いても、金融資産を多く保有する家計と多額の住宅ローンを抱える家計は必ずしも一致しない点
だ。マクロでみた家計部門の資産超過幅の拡大を、そのまま個々の家計の金利上昇への耐性向上
とみなすことはできない。

3．金利上昇の恩恵と負担は、誰に向かうのか 
経済財政白書2026 は、家計の資産・負債の保有構造を踏まえ、金利上昇の影響を家計の属性別
に分析している。金融資産は高齢層ほど保有額が大きい傾向にある一方、住宅ローンは住宅取得
0

年度末

### Page 3

3/4 
期の現役世代で多い。こうした保有構造の違いによって、金利上昇による受取利子と支払利子へ
の影響も世代によって異なる。 
白書では、2023 年から2026 年にかけての実際の金利変化を参考に、住宅ローンなどの負債にか
かる金利が1％ポイント、預金等の金融資産にかかる金利が0.6％ポイント上昇するケースを試算
している（資料2）。この前提には、近年の金利上昇局面で、資産側の受取利子率の上昇幅が負債
側の支払利子率を下回ってきたという非対称性が反映されている。したがって、資料2 は長期金
利3％の直接的な影響ではなく、近年の実績を踏まえた一定の金利上昇による影響を示したもので
ある。

資料 2 金利上昇の世代別影響～純受取利子の増減の内訳 
（右図は、世代別・住宅ローン有無別でみた影響）

（注）金融経済教育推進機構「家計の金融行動に関する世論調査」に基づき内閣府が試算。2024 年の二人以上世帯の金融資
産・負債をベースに、金融資産のうち株式・投資信託を除く資産には受取利子率の上昇幅を、負債総額には支払利子率の上昇
幅をそれぞれ乗じて算出。2023 年から 2026 年にかけての貸出金利と定期預金金利の上昇幅を参考に、負債にかかる金利が
1％ポイント、預金等の金融資産にかかる金利が 0.6％ポイント上昇すると仮定。 
（出所）内閣府「令和 8 年度年次経済財政報告」より抜粋

試算によると、20～40 代では支払利子の増加が受取利子の増加を上回るのに対し、60～70 代で
は受取利子の増加の方が大きい（資料 2 左図）。とりわけ影響が大きいのが、住宅ローンを保有す
る30 代だ（資料2 右図）。年間の負担増は平均24 万円程度と、平均可処分所得（約 600 万円）の
4％程度に相当する。さらに、30 代の住宅ローン保有世帯の6 割強では、負担増が年間20 万円を
超える。金融資産の蓄積途上にある一方、住宅ローン残高が相対的に大きいことが背景にあると
考えられる。 
この試算を資料1 に示した長期的なバランスシートの変化と重ね合わせると、家計部門全体の
金融資産が大幅に増え、資産超過幅も30 年前より拡大している。一方、こうしたマクロでみた家
計部門の「強さ」は、個々の家計の金利耐性とは必ずしも一致しないことがわかる。金利が動く
局面では、資産と負債をどの程度、どのような形で保有しているかが、家計収支を左右する重要
な要因となる。

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4

4．おわりに～「金利のある世界」が問い直す家計の資産・負債 
30 年ぶりの長期金利3％を、1996 年への回帰と捉えるのは適切ではない。金利が低下へ向かっ
ていた当時と、超低金利から正常化へ向かう現在では、同じ3％でも意味が異なる。そして、その
金利を受け止める家計の姿も大きく変わった。 
では、家計は30 年前より強くなったのか。金融資産・負債の構成からみた金利上昇への財務的
な耐性という意味では、家計部門全体のバランスシートは30 年前より厚みを増したといえる。金
融資産は大幅に増加するなか、負債総額は概ね横ばいで推移し、マクロでみた資産超過幅は大き
く拡大したからだ。しかし、その強さは個々の家計に均等に及んでいるわけではない。金融資産
は高齢層ほど保有額が大きい一方、住宅ローンを保有する世帯は、現役世代で多い傾向にある。
もっとも、同じ世代でも住宅ローンの有無や金融資産の保有状況によって影響は大きく異なる。
金利上昇によって、多額の資産を保有する家計では受取利子の増加が期待できるのに対し、住宅
ローンを抱える世帯では支払利子の増加が家計収支を圧迫する可能性がある。結局のところ、家
計はマクロでは30 年前より強くなったものの、個々の家計の金利耐性には大きな差がある。 
長く続いた超低金利の下では、資産・負債の保有状況の違いは金利収支に表れにくかった。金
利が再び動く時代には、その違いが受取利子や支払利子を通じて金融所得や消費余力の差として
表れやすくなる。長期金利3％は、 「金利のある世界」への回帰とともに、家計部門全体の資産超
過だけでなく、家計間の金利耐性の違いが経済的な意味を持つ時代に入ったことを象徴してい
る。

以 上

【注釈】 
1) 「資金循環統計」の家計部門には個人事業主が含まれる。また、金融資産には現金・預金、株
式・投資信託等のほか、生命保険受給権や企業年金等に関する年金受給権なども含まれる。

这是德鲁肯⽶勒⾸次公开访韩，聚焦 AI 与能源两⼤主线，将考察掌握 HBM 、半导体材料等
AI 产业链瓶颈的三星、海⼒⼠，以及布局核电与燃⽓轮机的⽃⼭能源。值得注意的是，他
已将 AI 仓位削减⾄六个⽉前的 20% ，此⾏被解读为从⼴泛押注转向精准筛选核⼼标的的战
略调整。
摘要：
“ ⻉森特导师 ” 、全球宏观投资⼤佬 Stanley Druckenmiller 正式访问韩国，计划与三星电⼦、 SK 海
⼒⼠、⽃⼭等主要企业⾼管会⾯，探讨投资机会。这是他⾸次公开访问韩国，被市场视为韩国
企业在全球 AI 供应链中地位跃升的重要信号。
据韩国媒体 9 ⽉ 20 ⽇报道，韩国业界⼈⼠透露， Druckenmiller 最早将于 9 ⽉ 22 ⽇在⾸尔与⽃⼭株
式会社及⽃⼭能源（ Doosan Enerbility ）⾼管会⾯，此后还计划陆续拜访三星电⼦、 SK 海⼒
⼠、 Naver 及 Coupang 等主要 AI 相关企业。 Druckenmiller 今年 2 ⽉曾在接受摩根⼠丹利采访时表

⽰，他在韩国和⽇本市场持有 " ⼤规模仓位 " ，此次亲赴⾸尔被认为是在审视现有投资的同时，积
极寻找新的投资标的。
值得关注的是，此次访问恰逢 Druckenmiller 对 AI 投资态度明显趋于审慎之际。他在 9 ⽉ 10 ⽇纽
约⼀场投资者活动上表⽰，已将 AI ⾏业投资规模削减⾄六个⽉前的 20% 。分析⼈⼠认为，他亲赴
韩国 AI 企业的举动，意在对 AI 投资进⾏更精准的筛选，重点考察真正掌握 AI 产业链瓶颈环节的企
业。此次访问若促成实质性投资，可能引发更多全球重量级投资者相继赴韩，进⽽推动外界重
新评估韩国在全球产业供应链中的地位。
⾸次公开访韩，聚焦 AI 与能源两⼤主线
Druckenmiller 此次韩国之⾏围绕两条主线展开：AI 基础设施与能源。
在 AI 领域，三星电⼦和 SK 海⼒⼠是⾼带宽内存（ HBM ）的核⼼供应商，⽽ HBM 是 AI 芯⽚不可或
缺的关键组件。⽃⼭株式会社则在 AI 芯⽚基板所需的关键材料 —— 覆铜板（ CCL ， Copper

Clad Laminate ）领域拥有重要布局。
据报道，业界⼈⼠指出，随着 AI 基础设施投资持续扩张，韩国企业在内存、半导体材料、发电设
备及电⽹等关键瓶颈领域的重要性显著提升，这被认为是 Druckenmiller 此⾏的核⼼驱动⼒之
⼀。
在能源领域，⽃⼭能源的核⼼业务涵盖核电站主要设备及燃⽓轮机，与 Druckenmiller 近期持续
加码能源投资的⽅向⾼度契合。
报道称，据悉，其旗下 Duquesne Family Office 今年⼀季度斥资逾 1.27 亿美元买⼊阿根廷油⽓⽣
产商 YPF 股份，并新建了对墨西哥能源公司 Vista Energy 的投资仓位。市场⼈⼠预计，他将在与
⽃⼭⾼管会⾯后，评估是否进⼀步加⼤对韩国能源⾏业的投资。
AI 投资降仓背景下的精准筛选
Druckenmiller 此⾏的另⼀层深意在于，他正处于主动压缩 AI 整体敞⼝的阶段，却选择亲赴韩国
考察 AI 相关企业，这⼀反差引发市场⼴泛解读。

管理 52 亿美元资产，与美国核⼼决策层关系密切
Druckenmiller 是华尔街最具影响⼒的全球宏观投资者之⼀，以 1992 年做空英镑并获取巨额利润
⽽声名⼤噪。⽬前，他执掌管理个⼈及家族资产的 Duquesne Family Office ，该机构去年 6 ⽉向

美国证券交易委员会（ SEC ）申报的美国上市证券投资组合规模达 52 亿美元（约合 7.2 万亿韩
元）。
Druckenmiller 与美国核⼼政策圈关系深厚，被业界⼴泛描述为美国财政部⻓⻉森特（ Scott
Bessent ）的导师，美联储主席沃什（ Kevin Warsh ）亦曾在 Duquesne Family Office 任职。报
道称，⼀位⾦融业内⼈⼠表⽰， “Druckenmiller 的判断与市场展望对全球⾦融投资⾏业具有重⼤
影响⼒。 ”
正因如此，他的投资动向历来受到市场⾼度关注。业界⼈⼠表⽰， Druckenmiller 此次韩国之⾏
有望成为更多全球经济重量级⼈物相继访韩的催化剂，“ 如果更多全球商界领袖在访问韩国后扩
